# About this item

*    SPECIFICATIONS: This damaged bolt and stud extractor tool is 3/8-inch drive and can be used with manual or pneumatic impact wrenches and ratchets, quickly and easily removing damaged bolts and studs more efficiently than standard bolt extractors. This bolt extractor fits 1/4 - 1/2-inch (6 - 12mm) studs. 
*    DESIGN: Manufactured with high-grade heat-treated alloy steel for maximum durability, this tool also features a chrome finish for additional rust and corrosion protection, with a knurled grip for extra stability. The universal design of this bolt extractor clamps down on the damaged stud for stable and secure removal, and the hexagonal flat can also be used with flat wrenches, adjustable wrenches, sockets, and locking pliers. 
*    APPLICATION: Traditional bolt extractors force the user to guess the size of damaged studs, stripping damaged screws and shaving metal off without budging the fastener. This extractor eliminates guesswork by adjusting and clamping to the bolt, saving hours of frustration on broken exhaust manifolds, oil filler plugs, rounded starter bolts, low grade bolts left in saltwater, painted-over bolts, rusted tight bolts, and more. 
*    RELIABLE | EFFECTIVE | EFFICIENT: Users trust ARES to be quality tools that make an impact. You are backed by our Performance Assurance Limited Lifetime Warranty. If you have any issues with your ARES Damaged Bolt and Stud Extractor Tool, simply contact customer service for troubleshooting help, parts, replacement, or refund. 
*    ASPCA BUSINESS AMBASSADOR: We are proud to be part of the ASPCA Business Ambassador Program. If you have a pet, you realize they are more than a pet, they are part of the family. With your support, we’re proud to help this great organization."

Customer Reviews, including Product Star Ratings help customers to learn more about the product and decide whether it is the right product for them.

To calculate the overall star rating and percentage breakdown by star, we don’t use a simple average. Instead, our system considers things like how recent a review is and if the reviewer bought the item on Amazon. It also analyzed reviews to verify trustworthiness.

### Customers say

Customers find the extractor tool to be well-made and effective at removing stuck studs and broken bolts, with one customer reporting success with a Harley stripped head caliper bolt. The tool offers good value for money, saving work and money, and customers appreciate its ease of use, with one noting it's easier than using lock pliers. The durability receives mixed feedback - while some say it's tough as any extractor, others report that four out of six units broke off during use. The grip performance and size are also mixed, with some saying it grips well while others find it too big for their needs.

_5 out of 5 stars_

##### Works as it should

We used this to weld onto the end of a metal T handle about 4 feet long to open and close our swimming pool. That whole bending over while turning an Allen wrench is ridiculous so I got this to hold the correct Allen size key and no more bending over. This is particularly nice in the springtime because you get a lot more torque getting the anchor screws to go down past the safety cover recoil Spring.

Hawkins](https://www.amazon.com/gp/profile/amzn1.account.AEZY5U4BWCD6552SU4D5O44RSCRA?ref=cm_cr_dp_d_bdcrb_top) _5 out of 5 stars_ ##### [Surprisingly tough!](https://www.amazon.com/portal/customer-reviews/srp/-/R1E27WA0FIV8UG/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_ttl?_encoding=UTF8&ie=UTF8)Reviewed in the United States on December 29, 2018 [Verified Purchase](https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_avp?ie=UTF8&nodeId=G8UYX7LALQC8V9KA)  Brief content visible, double tap to read full content. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. This thing is surprisingly good at what it's for. I didn't have a lot of expectations for these cheap internet brands like Ares, Tekton, etc., but this thing is tough and handles a huge amount of torque.

I am a diesel tech and the first time I used this extractor was to remove 12mm studs on a road-salt truck. These salt trucks get so rusty and beat up, the studs were practically welded in place. I used this tool in conjunction with a long 1/2" ratchet with a 2 foot cheater pipe on it to remove the studs. They were a BEAR to get out, but this tool didn't slip once. The harder you pull, the tighter it grabs. I was very impressed.

Now, keep in mind, this does severely damage the threads of the stud you're pulling, so I wouldn't use it one something I needed to re-use the studs with. Also, it is a little bit large and so you do need some clearance to use it. Don't count on it working in tight spaces, or where the stud is within maybe 3/4 inch of an obstruction. Further, it did give significant wear on the inner pieces that grab, but I've used it since and still works fine. Hopefully when I do need to replace it, the company will be easy to deal with concerning a warranty because it does supposedly have a lifetime guarantee (but I've learned some companies are just all talk - here's looking at you, Grey Pneumatic). Even if they don't honor that though, I'd still probably buy it again at this price point. *   [![Image 116](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif) lightwolf](https://www.amazon.com/gp/profile/amzn1.account.AFSE5HQGDC2X5GGDPFYXPYT5TGYA?ref=cm_cr_dp_d_bdcrb_top) _4 out of 5 stars_ ##### [Great, but not for 6mm](https://www.amazon.com/portal/customer-reviews/srp/-/RG6JOZET72EG8/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_ttl?_encoding=UTF8&ie=UTF8)Reviewed in the United States on October 15, 2017 [Verified Purchase](https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_avp?ie=UTF8&nodeId=G8UYX7LALQC8V9KA)  Brief content visible, double tap to read full content. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. I refer to this as "the beast" when I have to extract something. It has not been beaten in my use. This is a heavy duty tool that works very well. If it can get a hold on the stud you are extracting, it should get it out. This works just like a drill chuck, and there is one of its limitations. At the smaller sizes, such as 1/4", the jaws extend beyond the housing so it can acquire studs broken close to whatever they are embedded in. At the larger sizes, the jaws are within the housing, so it will require more stud to capture it. The jaws go flush to the housing at around 10mm. It is also large and heavy, and so can be awkward to use. Overall though, it is a clear winner.

For those working with metric studs, this WILL NOT EXTRACT 6MM STUDS, even though product description says it will. 1/4" is converted to 6mm, but the rounding error here is very significant. 1/4" = 6.35mm and that 1/3mm is the difference between this working and not. It will grip a 1/4" stud, but NOT 6mm. If you need a 6mm pulled, Lisle makes a set of good extractors. *   [![Image 118](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif) DesertLife](https://www.amazon.com/gp/profile/amzn1.account.AGJUVUEIALXHTPNNIUKKWRPT6JEA?ref=cm_cr_dp_d_bdcrb_top) _5 out of 5 stars_ ##### [Worth its weight in gold!](https://www.amazon.com/portal/customer-reviews/srp/-/R54CSML1JPEFK/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_ttl?_encoding=UTF8&ie=UTF8)Reviewed in the United States on October 10, 2022 [Verified Purchase](https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_avp?ie=UTF8&nodeId=G8UYX7LALQC8V9KA)  Brief content visible, double tap to read full content. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. I had to remove head studs from a 40+year old Atomic 4 boat engine. When the double-nut trick wouldn't work, I needed an extractor. This thing is worth its weight in gold! Gripped the stud quickly and didn't slip. If it does start to slip, it automatically tightens. Just be sure that it's as tight as you can get it before starting the extraction process because if it slip, it can cause damage to the threads. This tool does put little "bite marks" on the stud being removed but I chased the threads afterwards and it worked fine. You'll know what I mean because if you're buying this tool, you likely have a good grasp of mechanics. I ruined exactly zero studs with this tool. Highly suggest you liberally soak threads with a penetrating lube, and then tap the top of the stud lightly with a hammer to shock the lube down into the stud threads. Wait, and repeat. If not, you'll run the risk of snapping the stud which is NOT the cause of this tool. And when you start to remove the stud with this tool, I highly suggest you continue to add lube down in the threads. If they're really frozen in there, you are advised to proceed with an "unscrew 1/4 turn, then tighten 1/8 turn" process, and so on, until the stud is fully removed. Again, a fairly priced, specialized tool that's worth it's weight in gold. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. I bought this to remove the studs from my exhaust manifold to y pipe on a 30 year old pickup. I had to remove the headers to make room for the tool and was fortunate that the never seize was still doing it's job after all this time on the header bolts. The studs, though, were half rotted and I was only able to remove a couple nuts and the rest broke off. With this tool, I was able to remove three of the six studs with a 1/2" ratchet and four foot helper bar. I tried with my 1/2" Stubby but it didn't budge and the retaining ring from the bolt extractor broke when I used that. The tool held up well through quite a bit of torque though, I just think it didn't like the impact wrench.

I did prep with a couple applications of PB Blaster and Seafoam Deep Creep. If I were to do it again, I'd probably hit it a couple more times and put the torch on it but I was all out of gas. Doing so might have saved me the trouble of having to drill out the other three studs.

My only concern is that the outer metal is very soft which isn't a big deal as it's not a contact part but it got pretty nicked up from the one use. Also, the retaining ring broke easily but it just seems to keep the two pieces from coming apart so I don't think it matters. All in all, this is a great tool to keep in the toolbox and I see it being used quite a bit on my old truck. Bonds](https://www.amazon.com/gp/profile/amzn1.account.AEAT4TOWG2AVOXLILLHM6BYYGXQA?ref=cm_cr_dp_d_bdcrb_top) _3 out of 5 stars_ ##### [Works, sort of](https://www.amazon.com/portal/customer-reviews/srp/-/RK4YNYI4SSLCS/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_ttl?_encoding=UTF8&ie=UTF8)Reviewed in the United States on June 11, 2020 [Verified Purchase](https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_rvw_avp?ie=UTF8&nodeId=G8UYX7LALQC8V9KA)  Brief content visible, double tap to read full content. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. As many others have found, it is only of marginal use for smaller studs. I needed to remove some 1/4" exhaust studs that had been eroded by the heat, and when I first tried the unit it would not close enough to get any grip at all. Fortunately if you look at it carefully you will see that the grippers are tapered somewhat. I disassembled the tool (pop out the circlip on the top and remove the grippers and springs). I then ground the sides of the grippers a little to allow them to move closer together. When I reassembled the unit the tightening nut (black) bottomed out. I disassembled one again and put a washer on top of the grippers to allow the nut to push them out a little more. This did the trick.

Of course, most of the studs ended up breaking, but that's how it goes.

The manufacturer should either slim the grippers up a little bit or stop claiming it works on 1/4" studs. Full content visible, double tap to read brief content. Frigging phenomenal!!!!

I tried to remove a stud from the top of the rear engine mount in my 96 Camry for three weekends using two stop nuts unsuccessfully.

The mount and the stud on it are located between engine and firewall under the air intake manifold and above a bunch of other drive train stuff. Short version you can’t see it let alone get a normal tool on it.

After three weekends of frustration I ordered this tool. It removed the stud in 20 minutes. That includes jacking up the engine to take weight off the motor mount. 20 minutes.

The tool left thin vertical score marks in threads but I bet I could have reused the stud if I just gave it a quick wire brush or ran a nut down it from the hardware store to clean threads. Super minor impact on thread condition. Didn’t nead to because motor mount included a new stud.

Also. Used it repeatedly after to uninstall and reinstall the mount several times as I got the engibe properly positioned. On those runs it acted as a hand hold to turn the new stud that came with the mount. No scoring at that torque level. Installed the stud with di-electric grease on threads so it didnt lock up but it was too tough to finger turn. However it came out easily by finger tightening this tool on it and turning by hand.

The top black nut is 15/16 and the silver hex on the chucknpart seems to be about 1 3/8. I used an adjustable and my hand on that part so the measure is what I got with a tape measure.

Totally worth it if you have a hard to get at stud to remove or even an easy one and don’t feel like wasting time. # Product Summary: ARES 70016 Damaged Bolt and Stud Extractor Tool - Grips 1/4 to 1/2-In | Ideal for broken, rounded off, painted over, and rusted tight bolts

From ARES

4.4 out of 5 stars, 3,034 ratings

[Customer reviews](https://www.amazon.com/dp/B01GKEBG9I?ref=dp_product_quick_view#customer-reviews_feature_div)
100+ viewed in past month

## Price

One-time purchase: $21.56

[Purchasing options](https://www.amazon.com/dp/B01GKEBG9I?ref=dp_product_quick_view#buybox)

[Other sellers](https://www.amazon.com/dp/B01GKEBG9I?ref=dp_product_quick_view#dynamic-aod-ingress-box)

[Other sellers](https://www.amazon.com/dp/B01GKEBG9I?ref=dp_product_quick_view#unqualifiedBuyBox)

## About this Item

*   SPECIFICATIONS: This damaged bolt and stud extractor tool is 3/8-inch drive and can be used with manual or pneumatic impact wrenches and ratchets, quickly and easily removing damaged bolts and studs more efficiently than standard bolt extractors. This bolt extractor fits 1/4 - 1/2-inch (6 - 12mm) studs.
*   DESIGN: Manufactured with high-grade heat-treated alloy steel for maximum durability, this tool also features a chrome finish for additional rust and corrosion protection, with a knurled grip for extra stability. The universal design of this bolt extractor clamps down on the damaged stud for stable and secure removal, and the hexagonal flat can also be used with flat wrenches, adjustable wrenches, sockets, and locking pliers.
*   APPLICATION: Traditional bolt extractors force the user to guess the size of damaged studs, stripping damaged screws and shaving metal off without budging the fastener. This extractor eliminates guesswork by adjusting and clamping to the bolt, saving hours of frustration on broken exhaust manifolds, oil filler plugs, rounded starter bolts, low grade bolts left in saltwater, painted-over bolts, rusted tight bolts, and more.
*   RELIABLE | EFFECTIVE | EFFICIENT: Users trust ARES to be quality tools that make an impact. You are backed by our Performance Assurance Limited Lifetime Warranty. If you have any issues with your ARES Damaged Bolt and Stud Extractor Tool, simply contact customer service for troubleshooting help, parts, replacement, or refund.
*   ASPCA BUSINESS AMBASSADOR: We are proud to be part of the ASPCA Business Ambassador Program. If you have a pet, you realize they are more than a pet, they are part of the family. With your support, we’re proud to help this great organization."

## Product Description

 Eliminate The Guesswork The ARES Damaged Bolt And Stud Extractor Tool has a universal design that allows you to clamp down on damaged studs from 1/4-inch to 1/2-inch (6 - 12mm), taking the guess work out of extracting them. This tool has a 3/8-inch drive that can be used with hand ratchets, impact wrenches and air ratchets, and the hexagonal flat can be used with flat wrenches, adjustable wrenches, sockets, and locking pliers. Its compact design allows access into small areas, where studs may be hard to reach, and it also offers knurling for grip. Manufactured with high-grade, Heat-Treated Alloy Steel for maximum durability and chrome finish for additional rust protection, this extractor is perfect for broken exhaust manifolds, oil filler plugs, rounded starter bolts, low grade bolts left in saltwater, and more! Download manual > Features: Includes: 1 pc. bolt extractor 3/8-inch square drive for use with manual or pneumatic impact wrenches and ratchets Hexagonal flats for use with flat wrenches, pliers, adjustable wrenches, sockets and locking pliers Fits 1/4-inch to 1/2-inch (6 - 12mm) studs Universal design fits all types of fasteners Constructed of high-grade, heat-treated alloy steel for maximum durability Chrome finish for additional rust protection Compact design allows access into small areas Breaks free rounded-off, rusted-tight or painted-over bolts What Our Customers Are Saying: "Excellent tool. Worked like a charm to extract 2 forty year old exhaust manifold studs (10mm dia,) on an aluminum motorcycle cylinder! I am looking around for other studs to pull out now just for fun ... :-)" "Outstanding product. I removed 3 broken exhaust manifold studs in less than an hour." "This thing is a beast. I would have saved myself AT LEAST four hours of uninstall/install time on the driver's side catalytic converter on my wife's Pathfinder if I had this thing to begin with. Since I didn't have this, I had to remove the driver's side exhaust manifold from the engine in order to remove the top stud bolt (had to drill it out instead). I wound up ordering this AFTER I did the first catalytic converter uninstall/install. I wasn't about to go through the same crap on the passenger side as I did the driver's side."
